            buffer_bytes.push_back((size_t) sizes[i]);
            spans.emplace_back(at, sizes[i]);
        }
        std::sort(spans.begin(), spans.end());
        for (size_t i = 1; i < spans.size(); ++i)
            if (spans[i - 1].first + spans[i - 1].second > spans[i].first) {
                error = "invalid or duplicate resident slot"; return false;
            }
        std::vector<size_t> spares(classes.size() * count, kAbsent);
        uint64_t at = 0;
        for (size_t c = 0; c < classes.size(); ++c)
            for (size_t q = 0; q < count; ++q) {
                if (q >= count - bounce && c + 1 != classes.size()) continue;   // the bounce ordinals: the largest size only
                spares[c * count + q] = buffers.size();
                buffers.push_back({exchange_host + (size_t) at, exchange_device + (size_t) at});
                buffer_bytes.push_back(classes[c]);
                at += classes[c];
            }
        experts_ = std::move(experts);
        expert_count_ = offsets.size();
        expert_bytes_.assign(sizes.begin(), sizes.end());
        spares_.swap(spares);
        buffers_.swap(buffers);
        buffer_bytes_.swap(buffer_bytes);
        classes_ = classes;
        count_ = count;
        blob_bytes_ = classes.back();
        return true;
    }

    bool active() const { return blob_bytes_ != 0; }
    View resident(size_t expert) const {
        if (expert >= expert_count_) return {};
        const size_t slot = experts_[expert].load(std::memory_order_acquire);
        return slot != kAbsent ? buffers_[slot] : View{};
    }
    /// Spare `q` of the largest block size (any block fits it).
    View spare(size_t q) const { return active() ? spare(q, blob_bytes_) : View{}; }
    /// Spare `q` of blocks of exactly `bytes`.
    View spare(size_t q, size_t bytes) const {
        const size_t c = class_of(bytes);
        if (c == kAbsent || q >= count_) return {};
        const size_t id = spares_[c * count_ + q];
        return id != kAbsent ? buffers_[id] : View{};
    }

    // Only after every CPU reader and H2D copy of `in` has completed, and `out`
    // has landed in spare(q, bytes). No allocation, memcpy, or CUDA operation here.
    bool commit(size_t in, size_t out, size_t q, const uint8_t* staged, size_t bytes) {
        const size_t c = class_of(bytes);
        if (in >= expert_count_ || out >= expert_count_ || c == kAbsent || q >= count_ || !staged) return false;
        size_t& spare_id = spares_[c * count_ + q];
        const size_t incoming_slot = experts_[in].load(std::memory_order_acquire);
        if (spare_id == kAbsent || incoming_slot == kAbsent || experts_[out].load(std::memory_order_acquire) != kAbsent ||
            buffer_bytes_[incoming_slot] != bytes || expert_bytes_[out] != bytes || buffers_[spare_id].host != staged)
            return false;
        // A background router lookahead can query residency to skip redundant
        // file prefetches. Publish one atomic slot ID; the pointer/alias pair is
        // immutable. Actual compute readers still obey the completion contract.
        experts_[out].store(spare_id, std::memory_order_release);
        spare_id = incoming_slot;
        experts_[in].store(kAbsent, std::memory_order_release);
        ++exchanges_;
        avoided_bytes_ += bytes;
        return true;
    }

    uint64_t exchanges() const { return exchanges_; }
    uint64_t avoided_bytes() const { return avoided_bytes_; } // memcpy payload, not read+write total
    size_t block_sizes() const { return classes_.size(); }
    void clear() {
        experts_.reset(); expert_count_ = 0; expert_bytes_.clear(); buffers_.clear(); buffer_bytes_.clear(); spares_.clear();
        classes_.clear();
        count_ = 0; blob_bytes_ = 0; exchanges_ = avoided_bytes_ = 0;
    }

private:
    static constexpr size_t kAbsent = std::numeric_limits<size_t>::max();
    static std::vector<size_t> classes_of(const std::vector<uint64_t>& sizes) {
        std::vector<size_t> c;
        for (const uint64_t b : sizes)
            if (b != 0 && b <= std::numeric_limits<size_t>::max()) c.push_back((size_t) b);
        std::sort(c.begin(), c.end());
        c.erase(std::unique(c.begin(), c.end()), c.end());
        return c;
    }
    size_t class_of(size_t bytes) const {
        const auto it = std::lower_bound(classes_.begin(), classes_.end(), bytes);
        return it != classes_.end() && *it == bytes ? (size_t) (it - classes_.begin()) : kAbsent;
    }
    std::unique_ptr<std::atomic<size_t>[]> experts_;
    size_t expert_count_ = 0;
    std::vector<uint64_t> expert_bytes_; // each expert's block size, immutable
    std::vector<View> buffers_; // immutable physical addresses after initialize
    std::vector<size_t> buffer_bytes_; // each buffer's block size, immutable
    std::vector<size_t> spares_; // [size class * count_ + q]; touched only by the serialized cache manager
    std::vector<size_t> classes_; // the distinct block sizes, ascending
    size_t count_ = 0;
    size_t blob_bytes_ = 0; // the largest block size; 0 = not initialized
    uint64_t exchanges_ = 0, avoided_bytes_ = 0;
};

} // namespace strata::core::detail

// END FILE
// FILE: include/strata/core/expert_cache.hpp
// include/strata/core/expert_cache.hpp - R4: the VRAM-resident expert tier.
//
// **WHY THIS EXISTS, IN ONE PARAGRAPH.** The CPU expert pool is the engine's largest single cost: measured,
// **663.6 MB of expert bytes per token at ~40 GB/s = 16.2 ms**, on a token of ~53 ms, and `test_expert_pool`
// says the pipeline hides **1.055 ms of 19.035** - the CPU work is 96% exposed because the residual chain is
// strictly serial. The only way past it is to stop reading those bytes: put some of the 48 x 512 experts in
// VRAM and let the GPU compute them, so the CPU reads only the misses.
//
// **THE DESIGN IS SIZED AGAINST MEASUREMENTS, NOT AGAINST THE PLAN'S ASSUMPTIONS.**
//
//   * `h_expert` at 4,105 slots, **ten-fold leave-one-out: 0.6447 [0.6110, 0.6750]** (`tools/routing_kfold.py`).
//     The 0.6573 the README published is in-sample and 0.4720 measured a single-prompt CORPUS, not the metric.
//   * VRAM, measured on a live run rather than at startup: **6414 MiB free** with the engine at steady state,
//     against **5413 MiB** for 4,105 slots. It fits, with ~1 GB spare. `strata-device`'s planner agrees and says
//     FITS, but the planner runs before the dense arena exists, so the live figure is the one that counts.
//   * `h_layer` held-out is **0.0456** - only 4.6% of (layer, token) pairs have all ten experts resident - so
//     this cannot be a per-layer grouped kernel and the three-way split is not an optimisation.
//
// **WHAT THIS FILE IS.** The SLOT STORAGE and the RESIDENCY TABLE: it allocates the VRAM, fills it from the host
// arena, and answers `(layer, expert) -> slot or -1`. It computes nothing itself: the GPU computes the experts it
// holds in the verify window's grouped kernels and the prompt path (the hit/miss split), and the CPU pool the rest.
// (Written when nothing consumed the slots yet and `--expert-cache` defaulted to 0; setup's configs use `auto`.)
#pragma once

#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <memory>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

#include "strata/core/vmm.hpp"

namespace strata::core {

/// Where one expert's VRAM lives, or -1.  `int32_t` and not a bool because the slot index is what the kernel
/// needs, and a table that has to be re-derived from a bitmask is a table that will be re-derived differently
/// in the two places that use it.
inline constexpr int32_t kNotResident = -1;

/// **THE STATIC RESIDENCY PLAN, READ BACK FROM THE FILE `tools/make_profile.py` WROTE.**
///
/// `profile.bin` is `STRP`, then `version, n_layers, n_expert, slots, n_ranked`, then `n_ranked` `(layer,
/// expert)` pairs ranked by descending routing frequency, then a `n_layers x n_expert` frequency table.
///
/// **WHY A PROFILE AND NOT THE COMPULSORY-MISS POLICY THIS ENGINE ALREADY HAD.**  Compulsory-miss fills with
/// the first `S` DISTINCT experts the run happens to route, which on this workload is whatever the prompt
/// touched first - it measured `h = 0.4864`.  A profile ranked by frequency over a whole trace is what the
/// plan's `0.6447` refers to, and the CPU's half of the drain is linear in `h`, so the gap is worth about
/// 4 ms of drain.  The point of reading it from a file is that a profile built on ONE prompt can be scored on
/// ANOTHER, which is the only way to know whether it transfers.
///
/// The frequency table is not returned: it is the profile's own working, and the engine measures `h` by
/// running rather than by re-deriving what the file claims.
bool read_expert_profile(const std::string& path, int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert,
                         std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>>& ranked, int64_t& slots, std::string& err);

/// #477 (--expert-profile-save): what the adaptive tier learned, as a profile ranking EVERY (layer, expert) pair -
/// the experts resident in VRAM now first (where the swaps left the cache), then the rest; within each, by `heat`
/// (the routing the adaptive tier counted since the start, descending), then by `prior` (the profile the engine
/// started from: an expert this run never routed keeps its old place), then by index.  A start from it begins
/// where this one ended; one with more slots adds the hottest of the rest, one with fewer keeps the hottest.
/// `resident` and `heat`: n_layers x n_expert entries (`heat` may be empty: no counts, the prior decides).
std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>> rank_learned_profile(int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert,
                                                              const std::vector<uint8_t>& resident,
                                                              const std::vector<double>& heat,
                                                              const std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>>& prior);

/// #477: writes `ranked` in tools/make_profile.py's format, byte for byte: `STRP`, `1, n_layers, n_expert,
/// n_ranked, n_ranked` (uint32), the pairs (uint16 layer, uint16 expert), then the n_layers x n_expert int32 table
/// of each pair's rank (-1: not ranked).  Atomically: a `<path>.tmp` beside it, renamed over `path` once complete,
/// so a reader (the next start) never sees half a file.  False with `err` when it could not be written.
bool write_expert_profile(const std::string& path, int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert,
                          const std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>>& ranked, std::string& err);

/// Free device memory for the expert cache.  On a unified memory system - one memory for the CPU and the GPU, such as
/// the DGX Spark (GB10); CUDA reports it as cudaDevAttrIntegrated - cudaMemGetInfo counts only MemFree, not the page cache - which, with the experts read from the GGUF in place, is mostly those
/// files' clean pages and gives way to a device allocation.  There the figure is MemAvailable less 6 GiB for the OS
/// and the engine's host side (STRATA_UMA_HEADROOM_GIB).  Elsewhere it is cudaMemGetInfo's.  On Windows with an NVIDIA
/// card STRATA_VRAM_FREE=device (opt-in) takes the smaller of that and NVML's figure for the whole card: under WDDM
/// cudaMemGetInfo does not count what other processes hold.  It can only make the figure smaller.
size_t device_free_bytes();

/// One piece of an expert blob that lives contiguously in a source's own memory: `n` bytes at `p`, to land `at` bytes into the blob.
struct BlobPart { const uint8_t* p; uint64_t n; uint64_t at; };

class ExpertCache {
public:
    ExpertCache() = default;
    ~ExpertCache();
    ExpertCache(const ExpertCache&) = delete;
    ExpertCache& operator=(const ExpertCache&) = delete;

    /// Allocates `n_slots * blob_bytes` of DEVICE memory and a `n_layers * n_expert` residency table, and
    /// **CHECKS THE ALLOCATION AGAINST WHAT THE CARD ACTUALLY HAS** rather than assuming the planner was right.
    /// A cache that silently allocates less than it was asked for would report a hit rate for slots it does
    /// not have, which is the shape of error this project keeps paying for.
    bool open(int64_t n_slots, int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert, int64_t blob_bytes, std::string& err);
    /// Plan v0.3 P6: slots of the given sizes, back to back (a native pack's blobs differ per layer, and a
    /// profile-filled tier never moves an expert to another layer's slot, so each slot keeps its first size).
    bool open_sized(const std::vector<int64_t>& slot_bytes, int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert, std::string& err);
    /// Byte offset of each slot in the arena (null for uniform slots).
    const uint64_t* slot_offsets() const { return off_.empty() ? nullptr : off_.data(); }
    void close();

    bool valid() const { return base_ != nullptr; }
    /// The slots usable now: all of them, unless `shrink` gave the tail's VRAM back (#533) - then the ones wholly
    /// inside the VRAM still backed, always a prefix.  The prompt path's loan (the last slots) and every report
    /// follow this; `full_slots()` is what `open` allocated.
    int64_t slots() const { return live_slots_; }
    int64_t full_slots() const { return slots_; }
    /// Slots actually claimed.  Not the same as `slots()` - the cache does not evict, so a run that routes
    /// fewer distinct experts than there are slots leaves the rest empty.
    int64_t resident() const { return per_layer_ ? admitted_ : next_free_; }
    /// The bytes of `slots()` (all of the arena unless shrunk); `full_bytes()`: of every slot.
    int64_t bytes() const { return slot_end(live_slots_); }
    int64_t full_bytes() const { return slot_end(slots_); }
    double gib() const { return (double) bytes() / 1073741824.0; }

    /// **#533: HOT VRAM RESIZE, OPT-IN (--vram-elastic).**  With a segment size set before `open`/`open_sized`, the
    /// arena is one reserved address range backed by physical segments of that size (CUDA virtual memory
    /// management) instead of one cudaMalloc, so `shrink` can give the tail's VRAM back to the driver - for another
    /// program - and `grow` can take it again, while every slot keeps its address (the captured graphs, the verify
    /// plan's pointers and the prompt path's loan all hold addresses into it).  0 (the default): one cudaMalloc, as
    /// always.  CUDA only: a HIP build refuses it at open.  The slots past `slots()` after a shrink must hold no
    /// resident expert and no loan: the CALLER evicts them first (they become CPU misses).
    void set_segment_bytes(int64_t seg_bytes) { seg_req_ = seg_bytes > 0 ? seg_bytes : 0; }
    /// STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT: `open_sized` leaves at least `g` bytes of room after each slot's blob (the prompt MMQ
    /// reads a little past a down matrix when it reads an expert in its slot).  Set before opening, like
    /// `set_segment_bytes`; `close` keeps it.  0 (the default): no guard.  The uniform `open` ignores it, and so does
    /// the SYCL engine's open_sized (its slots then have no guard, and `slot_tail` finds no room).
    void set_slot_guard(int64_t g) { guard_req_ = g > 0 ? g : 0; }
    /// The guard the open slots were laid out with: what `open_sized` applied (0 for uniform slots, when closed, and
    /// on SYCL, whose open_sized applies none).
    int64_t slot_guard() const { return guard_; }
    /// A sized slot's arena bytes: the blob plus the guard, 256-byte aligned.
    static int64_t slot_stride(int64_t blob, int64_t guard = 0) { return (blob + guard + 255) / 256 * 256; }
    bool segmented() const { return !segs_.empty(); }
    int64_t segment_bytes() const { return seg_; }
    /// Bytes of the arena backed by VRAM now (a whole number of segments; the arena when not segmented).
    int64_t mapped_bytes() const;
    /// Unmaps every segment past the first `keep_bytes` (rounded UP to a segment boundary): `slots()` becomes the
    /// slots wholly inside what stays.  Waits for the device first.  False with `err` when not segmented or a driver
    /// call failed.
    bool shrink(int64_t keep_bytes, std::string& err);
    /// Maps segments again up to `want_bytes` (rounded DOWN to a segment, at most the arena; the last, shorter
    /// segment only when `want_bytes` covers the arena).  Stops at the first segment the driver cannot back (false,
    /// `err`: what was mapped by then stays).  The new slots are empty until the caller fills them.
    bool grow(int64_t want_bytes, std::string& err);
    /// The number of leading slots that fit wholly inside the first `bytes` bytes.
    int64_t slots_within(int64_t bytes) const;
    /// The bytes the first `n` slots span.
    int64_t bytes_of(int64_t n) const { return slot_end(n < 0 ? 0 : n > slots_ ? slots_ : n); }

    /// `(layer, expert)` -> slot index, or `kNotResident`.  Bounds-checked: a bad layer or expert returns
    /// `kNotResident` rather than reading whatever is adjacent in the table.
    int32_t slot_of(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) const;
    /// Claims the next free slot for `(layer, expert)`.  Returns the slot, or `kNotResident` when the cache is
    /// full - **it never evicts**, because eviction policy is a measured question (`R4.1`'s LFU-decay vs LRU
    /// sweep) and a placeholder policy would set the hit rate that everything downstream is then sized against.
    int32_t admit(int64_t layer, int64_t expert);

    /// Publish a same-layer replacement after its slot copy has completed.
    void replace(int64_t layer, int32_t old_expert, int32_t new_expert) {
        auto& old = residency_[(size_t) layer * n_expert_ + old_expert];
        residency_[(size_t) layer * n_expert_ + new_expert] = old;
        old = kNotResident;
    }

    /// **R4.2g: GIVE EACH LAYER ITS OWN SLOTS.  ROUND 328 MEASURED WHY THE GLOBAL FORM CANNOT WORK.**
    ///
    /// `admit`'s original policy hands out slots in ARRIVAL ORDER from one counter shared by every layer
    /// (`if (next_free_ >= slots_) return kNotResident;`).  A position looks at 10 experts in each of 48
    /// layers, so the first `n_slots` distinct (layer, expert) pairs are **about 26 layers of POSITION 0** -
    /// the cache fills entirely inside the first position it ever sees and never changes again, so hits are
    /// confined to those few layers for the rest of the run.
    ///
    /// Measured, `--expert-cache 256`: **1781 of 60000 = 2.97%**.  `Memory/cache_allocation.py` scores the
    /// same run's routing and says **8 slots per layer is worth 21.4%** and 64 is worth **70.4%**.  The cache
    /// was never short of capacity - it was handing all of it to two layers.
    ///
    /// With this on, layer `l` may only use slots `[l*q, (l+1)*q)` where `q = slots()/n_layers`, the last
    /// layer taking the remainder.  **EVICTION IS STILL ABSENT** - the property the comment above calls
    /// deliberate is preserved exactly; only WHICH slot a layer may take changes.  Default off, so every
    /// caller that predates this flag is bit-for-bit unaffected.
    void set_per_layer_admission(bool on) { per_layer_ = on; }
    bool per_layer_admission() const { return per_layer_; }
    /// The slot range layer `l` may admit into under per-layer admission.  Exposed so a test can check it.
    void layer_slot_range(int64_t layer, int64_t& lo, int64_t& hi) const;

    /// The device address of one slot.
    uint8_t* device_slot(int32_t slot);
    const uint8_t* device_slot(int32_t slot) const;

    /// Copies `(layer, expert)`'s blob from `host_blob` into `slot` on `stream`.  Asynchronous: the caller
    /// orders it.  Returns false if the indices are out of range rather than reading past the arena.
    /// `bytes` (plan v0.3 P6): the blob's own size when it is smaller than the slot (a native pack); 0 = the slot.
    bool fill_slot(int32_t slot, const uint8_t* host_blob, void* stream, std::string& err, int64_t bytes = 0);

    /// **THE SAME COPY, BUT BLOCKING, AND THE PROFILE FILL NEEDS IT.**  `fill_slot` is asynchronous because on
    /// the token path the fill and the kernel that reads it are ordered by one stream and waiting would be a
    /// synchronisation per layer.  At STARTUP there is no such ordering to lean on: the source is pageable host
    /// memory, `verify_slot` reads the slot back on the LEGACY stream, and a legacy-stream copy is not ordered
    /// against a `cudaStreamNonBlocking` one.  The first version used the async form and `verify_slot` refused
    /// the whole run with "slot 0 differs from the arena at byte 0" - which is the check doing its job.
    bool fill_slot_blocking(int32_t slot, const uint8_t* host_blob, std::string& err, int64_t bytes = 0);
    /// perf-review D-4: `fill_slot_blocking`'s copy on the same (legacy) stream, but queued: many slots are refilled
    /// with one `sync_queued` at the end instead of a wait per slot. Same ordering against earlier work, same bytes.
    bool fill_slot_queued(int32_t slot, const uint8_t* host_blob, std::string& err, int64_t bytes = 0);
    /// `fill_slot_queued` for a blob that is not in one piece: each `parts[i].n` bytes at `parts[i].p` go to byte `parts[i].at`
    /// of the slot (the pieces tile the blob).  Same queue, same `sync_queued`.
    bool fill_slot_queued_parts(int32_t slot, const BlobPart* parts, int n, std::string& err);
    bool sync_queued(std::string& err);

    /// Reads `slot` back to the host and compares it to `host_blob`, byte for byte.  **THE ONLY THING THAT SAYS
    /// THE CACHE HOLDS THE EXPERT IT CLAIMS TO.**  A slot table that is right about indices and wrong about
    /// bytes produces a plausible token, which is exactly the failure this project has paid for most often.
    bool verify_slot(int32_t slot, const uint8_t* host_blob, std::string& err, int64_t bytes = 0);

    /// Slots filled so far, for the startup report.
    int64_t fills() const { return fills_; }

    /// STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE=1 (strata/core/expert_nibble.hpp): called once the startup fill has written every slot
    /// (some of them as 4-bit codes, not GGUF blocks) and checked them.  From then on no slot may be written through
    /// this class: the fill_slot calls stop the engine (they would write GGUF bytes, possibly into a 4-bit slot, and a
    /// wrong expert gives a plausible token, never an error), and `admit` claims nothing.  Never called with the switch
    /// off: an unsealed cache is the one above.
    void seal() { sealed_ = true; }
    bool sealed() const { return sealed_; }

    /// The elastic K/V (--kv-grow): the arena in a VMM range (vmm.hpp) instead of one cudaMalloc, so the K/V can take
    /// single chunks of it and give them back. Applies to the next `open`; ignored where VMM is not available.
    static void set_vmm(bool enabled);
    /// The arena's range (null: one cudaMalloc).
    VmmRange* vmm_range() { return vmm_.get(); }
    /// Byte offset of slot `s` in the arena (s == slots(): the end).
    uint64_t slot_offset(int64_t s) const { return off_.empty() ? (uint64_t) s * (uint64_t) blob_ : off_[(size_t) s]; }
    /// The arena bytes of slot `s` (its blob, any slack and the guard).
    uint64_t slot_room(int32_t s) const { return slot_offset(s + 1) - slot_offset(s); }
    /// STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT: the address `blob` bytes into slot `s` when the slot has room for `need` more bytes there
    /// (null otherwise, or for a bad slot).  Uniform slots too: their stride is the largest blob.
    uint8_t* slot_tail(int32_t s, uint64_t blob, uint64_t need) const {
        if (s < 0 || s >= slots_ || base_ == nullptr) return nullptr;
        return slot_room(s) >= blob + need ? base_ + slot_offset(s) + blob : nullptr;
    }

private:
#if defined(STRATA_USE_HIP)
    bool ensure_blocking_staging(std::size_t bytes, std::string& err);
    uint8_t* blocking_staging_ = nullptr;
    std::size_t blocking_staging_bytes_ = 0;
#endif
    int64_t slot_end(int64_t n) const { return off_.empty() ? n * blob_ : (int64_t) off_[(size_t) n]; }
    bool open_segmented(uint64_t want, std::string& err);
    void release_segmented();
    uint8_t* base_ = nullptr;
    int64_t live_slots_ = 0;            ///< #533: slots() - all of them unless shrunk
    int64_t seg_req_ = 0;               ///< #533: the segment size asked for (0: one cudaMalloc)
    int64_t guard_req_ = 0;             ///< STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT: bytes kept after each sized slot's blob (set_slot_guard)
    int64_t guard_ = 0;                 ///< ...the guard open_sized applied to the open slots (0: none)
    int64_t seg_ = 0;                   ///< #533: the segment size used (a multiple of the driver's granularity)
    uint64_t reserved_ = 0;             ///< #533: the reserved address range's size
    std::vector<unsigned long long> segs_;   ///< #533: each segment's physical handle (0: unmapped)
    std::vector<int64_t> seg_size_;     ///< #533: each segment's size (the last one may be shorter)
    int64_t mapped_segs_ = 0;           ///< #533: segments [0, mapped_segs_) are backed
    std::unique_ptr<VmmRange> vmm_;    ///< the arena's range when it is in VMM (set_vmm)
    std::vector<int32_t> residency_;   ///< [n_layers * n_expert] -> slot or kNotResident
    int64_t slots_ = 0;
    int64_t n_layers_ = 0;
    int64_t n_expert_ = 0;
    int64_t blob_ = 0;
    int64_t next_free_ = 0;
    int64_t fills_ = 0;
    /// R4.2g.  `per_layer_` off (the default) leaves `next_free_` as the only admission counter, so the
    /// pre-existing path is untouched.
    bool per_layer_ = false;
    std::vector<int32_t> layer_next_;   ///< [n_layers] -> that layer's next free slot
    std::vector<uint64_t> off_;         ///< plan v0.3 P6: slot offsets (slots + 1 entries) when sized
    int64_t admitted_ = 0;
    bool sealed_ = false;               ///< STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE: `seal` was called (no slot is written after it)
};

}  // namespace strata::core

// END FILE
// FILE: include/strata/core/expert_nibble.hpp
// include/strata/core/expert_nibble.hpp - STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE=1 (opt-in, default off): the gate/up experts of
// IQ2_S / IQ3_XXS / IQ3_S layers kept as 4-bit codes in their VRAM slots, for Pascal GP100 (sm_60).
//
// **WHY.**  On a GP100 the decode window's gate/up expert kernels are not held by memory bandwidth: they use a small
// part of it and wait on each block's serial chain (the weights, the codebook lookups, the emulated dp4a, the
// reduction).  Re-encoding each 32-weight item of these three formats as sixteen bytes of 4-bit codes (one aligned
// 16-byte load) and keeping the integer scale and the fp16 d as they are makes the slot 1.25-1.68x the GGUF bytes and
// the decode of four weights 1-4 integer instructions instead of two codebook lookups.
//
// **WHY IT IS LOSSLESS.**  Their grids have only 3 (IQ2_S) or 8 (IQ3_XXS, IQ3_S) magnitudes, so each int8 weight the
// production `Split<TG>::load` decodes is one of at most 16 signed values.  At every start the transcoder decodes every
// item of every converted slot with the production load, encodes it, decodes the written code again and compares; and
// the GGUF blocks are rebuilt from every slot and compared with the blob byte for byte (`check_gguf`).  The decode
// kernels run the production integer chain and float tail in the production order: every gate / up value is the
// production kernel's, bit for bit.  The prompt path's MMQ reads the GGUF blocks rebuilt from the slot (`gather_gguf`,
// in place of the copy it does today): the same bytes, so the same bits.
//
// **OFF IS TODAY.**  Unset or 0: no layer is converted, `slot_bytes(l)` is `expert_layout().blob_bytes(l)`, nothing is
// transcoded, sealed or hashed, and none of the new branches is entered.  Only a CUDA build with
// STRATA_EXPERIMENTAL_SM60 has the kernels (STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_BUILT); every other build refuses the switch at start.
#pragma once

#include "strata/kernels/cpu/expert_layout.hpp"

#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstdlib>
#include <cstring>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

#if defined(STRATA_EXPERIMENTAL_SM60) && !defined(__HIPCC__) && !defined(STRATA_USE_HIP) && !defined(STRATA_HIP_GFX906) && \
    !defined(STRATA_GPU_METAL)
#define STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_BUILT 1
#else
#define STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_BUILT 0
#endif

#if defined(__CUDACC__) || defined(__HIPCC__)
#define STRATA_NIB_HD __host__ __device__
#else
#define STRATA_NIB_HD
#endif

namespace strata::core::nibble {

// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the slot layout
//
// A converted layer's expert slot, in order (the host arena and the pack keep the GGUF blocks):
//
//   codes   kRows x kItems x 16 B   row r, item k at (r * kItems + k) * 16; rows 0..kNFf-1 gate, kNFf..kRows-1 up
//   scales  kRows x kItems x 1 B    row r, item k at kScOff + r * kItems + k (the production scale step's input)
//   d       kRows x kSuper x 2 B    row r, superblock s at kDOff + (r * kSuper + s) * 2: the block's fp16 d, bit for bit
//   ------  kGuBytes = 1,766,400 B (6900 x 256: the down part starts 256-aligned)
//   down    the GGUF down rows, copied verbatim from the raw blob's [down_off, bytes)
//   ------  slot_bytes = round_up(kGuBytes + down bytes, kSlotAlign)
//
// One item = 32 weights of a row, one 16-byte code (four 32-bit words, see `encode_item`).  Only the expert shape
// n_embd 2560, n_ff 640 is converted: every other layer keeps its GGUF blob and its kernels.

inline constexpr int64_t kNEmbd = 2560;                 ///< the gate/up rows' width (the activation)
inline constexpr int64_t kNFf = 640;                    ///< gate rows = up rows = n_ff
inline constexpr int64_t kRows = 2 * kNFf;              ///< 1280: gate, then up
inline constexpr int64_t kItems = kNEmbd / 32;          ///< 80 items of 32 weights per row
inline constexpr int64_t kSuper = kNEmbd / 256;         ///< 10 superblocks (one fp16 d each) per row
inline constexpr uint64_t kItemBytes = 16;              ///< one item's code: four words, one 16-byte load

inline constexpr uint64_t kCodesOff = 0;
inline constexpr uint64_t kUpCodesOff = (uint64_t) kNFf * kItems * kItemBytes;          ///<   819,200: row kNFf
inline constexpr uint64_t kScOff = (uint64_t) kRows * kItems * kItemBytes;              ///< 1,638,400
inline constexpr uint64_t kDOff = kScOff + (uint64_t) kRows * kItems;                   ///< 1,740,800
inline constexpr uint64_t kGuBytes = kDOff + (uint64_t) kRows * kSuper * 2;             ///< 1,766,400
inline constexpr uint64_t kDownOff = kGuBytes;                                          ///< the down rows
inline constexpr uint64_t kSlotAlign = 256;                                             ///< every slot's size

static_assert(kGuBytes == 1766400, "the slot layout above");
static_assert(kGuBytes % kSlotAlign == 0, "the down part starts 256-aligned");
static_assert(kScOff % 16 == 0 && kDOff % 16 == 0, "each region starts 16-aligned");

/// Byte offset of row `row` (0..kRows-1; up rows are kNFf + r) item `k`'s code inside the slot.
STRATA_NIB_HD constexpr uint64_t code_off(int64_t row, int64_t k) {
    return kCodesOff + ((uint64_t) row * kItems + (uint64_t) k) * kItemBytes;
}
/// Byte offset of row `row` item `k`'s scale byte.
STRATA_NIB_HD constexpr uint64_t scale_off(int64_t row, int64_t k) { return kScOff + (uint64_t) row * kItems + (uint64_t) k; }
/// Byte offset of row `row` superblock `s`'s fp16 d (s = k / 8 for item k).
STRATA_NIB_HD constexpr uint64_t d_off(int64_t row, int64_t s) { return kDOff + ((uint64_t) row * kSuper + (uint64_t) s) * 2; }
/// A converted slot's size for a raw blob whose down rows are `down_bytes` long.
STRATA_NIB_HD constexpr uint64_t slot_bytes_for(uint64_t down_bytes) {
    return (kGuBytes + down_bytes + kSlotAlign - 1) / kSlotAlign * kSlotAlign;
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the formats

inline constexpr int kIQ3_XXS = 18;
inline constexpr int kIQ3_S = 21;
inline constexpr int kIQ2_S = 22;

/// Whether `gu_type` is converted (the three formats whose weights take at most 16 values).
STRATA_NIB_HD constexpr bool fmt_built(int gu_type) { return gu_type == kIQ2_S || gu_type == kIQ3_XXS || gu_type == kIQ3_S; }
/// The GGUF gate/up row bytes of `gu_type` at kNEmbd (a converted layer's raw blob must agree; 0 = not a format here).
STRATA_NIB_HD constexpr uint64_t raw_gu_row(int gu_type) {
    return gu_type == kIQ2_S ? 820 : gu_type == kIQ3_XXS ? 980 : gu_type == kIQ3_S ? 1100 : 0;
}
inline const char* fmt_name(int gu_type) {
    switch (gu_type) {
        case kIQ3_XXS: return "IQ3_XXS";
        case kIQ3_S: return "IQ3_S";
        case kIQ2_S: return "IQ2_S";
        default: return "?";
    }
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the item code
//
// `g[32]` below is one item as the production `Split<TG>::load` leaves it: g[4 * j + b] is byte b of W.g[j]
// (j = 0..7, the dp4a order against the q8_1 block's qs words).  Word m (0..3) of the 16-byte code holds W.g[2m]
// and W.g[2m + 1]:
//
//   IQ2_S    bits 4b..4b+3 = c(g[8m + b]), bits 16+4b.. = c(g[8m + 4 + b]), b = 0..3
//            c = magnitude index of {8, 25, 43} | sign << 2: the PRMT selector into {8,25,43,0} {-8,-25,-43,0}
//   IQ3_XXS  same nibble positions; c = index of {4, 12, 20, 28, 36, 44, 52, 62} | sign << 3
//   IQ3_S    byte b = u(g[8m + b]) | u(g[8m + 4 + b]) << 4, u = (v + 15) / 2 (v odd, -15..15); v = 2u - 15
//
// The scale byte keeps the production integer scale step's input:
//   IQ2_S    the block's scales byte for the item: ls0 | ls1 << 4 (W.ls0, W.ls1)
//   IQ3_XXS  aux32 >> 28 (0..15, W.ls)
//   IQ3_S    1 + 2 * nibble (1..31, W.ls)

/// Encodes one item; false when a weight is outside the format's code set (the conversion then refuses to start).
inline bool encode_item(int gu_type, const int8_t g[32], uint32_t q[4]) {
    bool ok = true;
    auto code = [&](int8_t v) -> uint32_t {
        const int a = v < 0 ? -(int) v : (int) v;
        if (gu_type == kIQ3_S) {
            if ((v & 1) == 0 || v < -15 || v > 15) { ok = false; return 0; }
            return (uint32_t) (((int) v + 15) / 2);
        }
        if (gu_type == kIQ3_XXS) {
            static constexpr int mag[8] = { 4, 12, 20, 28, 36, 44, 52, 62 };
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < 8; ++i)
                if (a == mag[i]) return i | (v < 0 ? 8u : 0u);
            ok = false;
            return 0;
        }
        const uint32_t m = a == 8 ? 0u : a == 25 ? 1u : a == 43 ? 2u : 3u;   // IQ2_S
        if (m == 3u) { ok = false; return 0; }
        return m | (v < 0 ? 4u : 0u);
    };
    for (int m = 0; m < 4; ++m) {
        uint32_t w = 0;
        for (int b = 0; b < 4; ++b) {
            const uint32_t c0 = code(g[8 * m + b]), c1 = code(g[8 * m + 4 + b]);
            if (gu_type == kIQ3_S) w |= (c0 | c1 << 4) << (8 * b);
            else w |= c0 << (4 * b) | c1 << (16 + 4 * b);
        }
        q[m] = w;
    }
    return ok;
}

/// Decodes one item's code back to the production W.g bytes (the host reference the parity program compares with;
/// the device decoders give the same bytes).  Codes outside the set decode as the PRMT does: IQ2_S index 3 -> 0.
inline void decode_item(int gu_type, const uint32_t q[4], int8_t g[32]) {
    static constexpr int8_t iq2[8] = { 8, 25, 43, 0, -8, -25, -43, 0 };
    static constexpr int8_t iq3[8] = { 4, 12, 20, 28, 36, 44, 52, 62 };
    for (int m = 0; m < 4; ++m)
        for (int b = 0; b < 4; ++b) {
            uint32_t c0, c1;
            if (gu_type == kIQ3_S) {
                c0 = q[m] >> (8 * b) & 15u;
                c1 = q[m] >> (8 * b + 4) & 15u;
            } else {
                c0 = q[m] >> (4 * b) & 15u;
                c1 = q[m] >> (16 + 4 * b) & 15u;
            }
            auto val = [&](uint32_t c) -> int8_t {
                if (gu_type == kIQ3_S) return (int8_t) (2 * (int) c - 15);
                if (gu_type == kIQ3_XXS) return (int8_t) ((c & 8u) ? -iq3[c & 7u] : iq3[c & 7u]);
                return iq2[c & 7u];
            };
            g[8 * m + b] = val(c0);
            g[8 * m + 4 + b] = val(c1);
        }
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the slot hash
//
// STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_RECHECK's fingerprint of a slot: over the slot's first `hashed` bytes (kGuBytes plus the down
// rows; the alignment tail is not written and not hashed) as little-endian 32-bit words w_i, the last one
// zero-extended,  H = sum_i (uint64) (w_i ^ i) * kHashMul  mod 2^64.  A sum, so the device's order of adding does not
// change it; the index term makes a moved word count.

inline constexpr uint64_t kHashMul = 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15ull;

/// The host reference of the slot hash (the device hash gives the same value for the same bytes).
inline uint64_t hash_host(const void* p, uint64_t bytes) {
    const uint8_t* s = (const uint8_t*) p;
    uint64_t h = 0;
    const uint64_t n = (bytes + 3) / 4;
    for (uint64_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        uint32_t w = 0;
        const uint64_t at = 4 * i, take = bytes - at < 4 ? bytes - at : 4;
        std::memcpy(&w, s + at, (size_t) take);   // little-endian hosts only
        h += (uint64_t) (w ^ (uint32_t) i) * kHashMul;
    }
    return h;
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the switches

struct Config {
    bool on = false;                       ///< STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE=1
    bool recheck = false;                  ///< STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_RECHECK=1 (debug: every slot's hash per request)
    std::vector<int> fmts{ kIQ2_S, kIQ3_XXS, kIQ3_S };   ///< STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_FMTS (default 22,18,21)
    std::vector<int64_t> skip;             ///< STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_SKIP: layers kept in their GGUF blocks
    std::string error;                     ///< non-empty: a malformed value - the start is refused
};

namespace detail {
/// "a,b,c" -> integers; false on anything else (empty = an empty list).
inline bool parse_list(const char* s, std::vector<int64_t>& out) {
    out.clear();
    while (s != nullptr && *s != '\0') {
        char* end = nullptr;
        const long long v = std::strtoll(s, &end, 10);
        if (end == s) return false;
        out.push_back((int64_t) v);
        s = end;
        if (*s == ',') ++s;
        else if (*s != '\0') return false;
    }
    return true;
}
}  // namespace detail

/// The switches, read once (generate.cpp reads them before anything is allocated).
inline const Config& config() {
    static const Config c = [] {
        Config r;
        const char* e = std::getenv("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE");
        if (e != nullptr && e[0] != '\0' && std::strcmp(e, "0") != 0) {
            if (std::strcmp(e, "1") == 0) r.on = true;
            else r.error = std::string("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE=") + e + ": expected 0 or 1";
        }
        const char* rc = std::getenv("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_RECHECK");
        r.recheck = rc != nullptr && std::strcmp(rc, "1") == 0;
        std::vector<int64_t> v;
        if (const char* f = std::getenv("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_FMTS"); f != nullptr && f[0] != '\0') {
            if (!detail::parse_list(f, v)) {
                if (r.error.empty()) r.error = std::string("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_FMTS=") + f + ": expected ggml types, e.g. 22,18,21";
            } else {
                r.fmts.clear();
                for (const int64_t t : v) {
                    if (!fmt_built((int) t) && r.error.empty())
                        r.error = "STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_FMTS: type " + std::to_string(t) +
                                  " is not converted (22 IQ2_S, 18 IQ3_XXS, 21 IQ3_S)";
                    r.fmts.push_back((int) t);
                }
            }
        }
        if (const char* k = std::getenv("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_SKIP"); k != nullptr && k[0] != '\0') {
            if (!detail::parse_list(k, r.skip) && r.error.empty())
                r.error = std::string("STRATA_EXPERT_NIBBLE_SKIP=") + k + ": expected layer numbers, e.g. 40,44";
        }
        return r;
    }();
    return c;
}

/// With the switch on, the first environment setting it refuses, as a message (empty = none): the paths that would
/// read a converted slot as GGUF blocks, write GGUF bytes into one, or run other kernels for the converted layers
/// than the ones the 4-bit kernels are bit for bit.  The command-line half (--peer-device, remote expert caches,
/// --kv-grow, --vram-elastic), the cc 6.0 check, full residency and no loan are generate.cpp's checks; the prompt
/// path's own (MMQ on every converted layer) is Prefill::nibble_prompt_check.
inline std::string env_conflicts() {
    auto set = [](const char* n) -> const char* {
        const char* v = std::getenv(n);
        return v != nullptr && v[0] != '\0' ? v : nullptr;
    };
    const char* v = set("STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT");
    if (v != nullptr && std::atoi(v) != 0)
        return "STRATA_MMQ_DIRECT (the prompt MMQ would read a converted slot in place as GGUF blocks)";
    v = set("STRATA_PREFILL_MMQ");
    if (v != nullptr && std::atoi(v) == 0) return "STRATA_PREFILL_MMQ=0 (the FP16 prompt path reads GGUF blocks)";
    v = set("STRATA_PF_FUSED");
    if (v != nullptr && v[0] == '1') return "STRATA_PF_FUSED=1 (the fused prompt kernels read GGUF blocks)";
    v = set("STRATA_FS_SLOTS");
    if (v != nullptr && std::atoi(v) > 0) return "STRATA_FS_SLOTS (Foresight swaps GGUF blobs into slots)";
    v = set("STRATA_EXPERT_V2");
    if (v != nullptr && v[0] == '1') return "STRATA_EXPERT_V2=1 (the S26 kernels, not the CUDA layout, on IQ3_S)";
    v = set("STRATA_KV_GROW");
    if (v != nullptr && v[0] != '0') return "STRATA_KV_GROW (the elastic K/V moves slots at their GGUF size)";
    v = set("STRATA_EXP_MODE");
    if (v != nullptr && std::atoi(v) != 0 && std::atoi(v) != 8)
        return "STRATA_EXP_MODE other than 0 or 8 (another layout's gate/up kernels)";
    return {};
}

// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the VRAM table

/// One layer's slots.  `on` false: the GGUF blob, `slot_bytes == raw_bytes`, the production kernels.
struct LayerVram {
    bool on = false;
    int gu_type = -1, d_type = -1;
    uint64_t down_off = 0;     ///< in the slot: kDownOff when on, else the raw blob's down_off
    uint64_t down_bytes = 0;   ///< the down rows (raw_bytes - raw_down_off)
    uint64_t hashed = 0;       ///< bytes the slot hash covers: kGuBytes + down_bytes when on, else 0
    uint64_t slot_bytes = 0;   ///< what the expert cache allocates per slot (`open_sized`, every estimate)
    // the raw blob as the arena and the pack hold it (expert_layout().fmt[l]); the transcoder reads these
    uint64_t raw_bytes = 0, raw_gu_row = 0, raw_up_off = 0, raw_down_off = 0;
};

/// One layer's entry from its GGUF format and the switches (pure; `plan_layers` calls it per layer).
inline LayerVram plan_layer(int64_t l, const strata::kernels::cpu::NativeFmt& f, const Config& c) {
    LayerVram v;
    v.gu_type = f.gu_type;
    v.d_type = f.d_type;
    v.raw_bytes = f.bytes;
    v.raw_gu_row = f.gu_row;
    v.raw_up_off = f.up_off;
    v.raw_down_off = f.down_off;
    v.down_bytes = f.bytes - f.down_off;
    v.down_off = f.down_off;
    v.slot_bytes = f.bytes;
    bool listed = false, skipped = false;
    for (const int t : c.fmts) listed = listed || t == f.gu_type;
    for (const int64_t s : c.skip) skipped = skipped || s == l;
    // [gate | up | down] exactly, no NVFP4 tail, the down rows a whole number of 16-byte words (the gathers copy them so)
    const bool shape = f.n_embd == kNEmbd && f.n_ff == kNFf && f.gu_row == raw_gu_row(f.gu_type) &&
                       f.up_off == (uint64_t) kNFf * f.gu_row && f
// END FILE
// FILE: include/strata/core/expert_source.hpp
// include/strata/core/expert_source.hpp - P2.S3/P2.S5: the expert pool's adapter to the host loop.
//
// `session_loop` publishes, per layer, the NORMED activation `x_f`, the ten routed expert ids and their router
// weights, and expects `k x n_embd` floats back.  This is the piece that turns those four things into an
// `ExpertPool::run` call.  Nothing here is clever, and that is the point: the pool, the kernel and the loop are
// each already verified, so this file has exactly one job - get the CONTRACT between them right.
//
// THE CONTRACT, and each clause is a way to be wrong:
//
//   1. **`x_f` IS THE SAME PINNED BUFFER EVERY LAYER.**  Its ADDRESS never changes, so the `ActQ` cannot be
//      cached by pointer - a cache keyed on `x_f` would quantize layer 0 and reuse it for all 47 remaining
//      layers, which is a finite, plausible, completely wrong token.  There is no cache here at all: the
//      conversion is one 2560-element pass against a 0.3 ms/layer budget, and a correct answer is worth more
//      than the microseconds.
//   2. **THE POOL DOES NOT APPLY THE ROUTER WEIGHT.**  `moe_combine` (`src/core/layer.cpp:479`) sums
//      `w[i] * parts[i]` on the device.  `ExpertJob::weight` is a diagnostic field; setting it here would
//      apply the weight twice, which is invisible in a single layer and compounds over 48.
//   3. **THE KERNEL'S GEOMETRY IS FIXED BY THE ARTIFACT** (`H = 2560`, `FF = 640`, `BLOB = 1,382,400`).  A
//      different `n_embd` or a different expert width is REFUSED rather than mis-indexed, because the blob's
//      internal offsets are compile-time constants and reading a 640-wide expert as a 2560-wide one walks off
//      the end into the next expert's bytes without faulting.
#pragma once

#include "strata/core/expert_cache.hpp"
#include "strata/core/pinned.hpp"
#include "strata/core/exchange_storage.hpp"
#include "strata/core/hit_hook.hpp"
#include "strata/kernels/cpu/pool.hpp"

#include <algorithm>
#include <array>
#include <cmath>
#include <atomic>
#include <chrono>
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstddef>
#include <condition_variable>
#include <deque>
#include <memory>
#include <mutex>
#include <thread>
#include <unordered_map>
#include <utility>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

struct ExpertLayout;
}

namespace strata::core {

class PeerExperts;   // multi-GPU: the second GPU's expert tier (peer_experts.hpp)
class RemoteExperts;
struct LoadStats;

namespace detail {

/// Sentinel used by the pure complement planner for a blob that remains in the mmap fallback.
inline constexpr uint64_t kNoCacheComplement = ~uint64_t{0};

/// Required cgroup-v2 usage counters for the conservative cache-reclaim allowance.
struct CgroupMemoryStat {
    uint64_t current = 0;
    uint64_t inactive_file = 0;
    uint64_t file_dirty = 0;
    uint64_t file_writeback = 0;
    bool valid = false;
    uint64_t file = 0;   ///< 149: memory.stat "file" (active + inactive page cache); 0 when not reported
};

/// Calculate additional bytes under a finite cgroup limit after reclaiming the group's clean page cache, as
/// MemAvailable counts it on a bare host: every clean file page (active and inactive: memory.stat "file", else
/// inactive_file), less a low-watermark share the kernel keeps (min(half the clean cache, limit / 64)).  A process
/// whose own reads (a GPU cache fill, a read-ahead) left gigabytes of active cache in its group was judged short of
/// RAM before (149: 1.2 GiB "available" of a 32 GiB group, the file tier then switched to unbuffered reads).
/// Returns false when the required memory.stat counters were unavailable.
bool cgroup_available_bytes(uint64_t limit, const CgroupMemoryStat& stat, uint64_t& bytes);

/// #633: the RAM this process can get.  `available`: MemAvailable (Windows: the available physical memory), lowered
/// to the room under the tightest cgroup limit; `cgroup_limit`: that tightest limit itself (v2 memory.max of the group
/// and its ancestors, or v1 memory.limit_in_bytes), ~0 when there is none - what a container can never exceed.
struct HostMemory {
    uint64_t available = 0;
    uint64_t cgroup_limit = ~uint64_t{0};
    uint64_t commit = ~uint64_t{0};   ///< available Windows commit capacity; ~0 when not reported
};

/// Linux reads `meminfo`, `self_cgroup` and the cgroup tree under `cgroup_root` (the parameters are for tests; the
/// defaults are the real files).  cgroup v2 as before (an unreadable limit of a group that has one fails); cgroup v1's
/// memory controller (`<root>/memory/<path>`: memory.limit_in_bytes - memory.usage_in_bytes); no cgroup line at all is
/// MemAvailable alone.  False when the RAM cannot be determined.
bool host_available_memory(HostMemory& m, const std::string& meminfo = "/proc/meminfo",
                           const std::string& self_cgroup = "/proc/self/cgroup",
                           const std::string& cgroup_root = "/sys/fs/cgroup");

/// Bound a resident budget by RAM and commit capacity after headroom; leave 256 MiB more when clamping.
/// Pass UINT64_MAX for commit when the platform does not report it.
uint64_t clamp_resident_budget(uint64_t requested, uint64_t physical, uint64_t commit, uint64_t headroom);

/// #1250: one cudaHostAlloc of the whole page-locked complement has no way back when the kernel does not give the
/// pages as fast as the driver takes them (the process is OOM-killed, even with MemAvailable high: clean file cache
/// that cannot be reclaimed at that moment).  `free_now` is MemFree (pages that are really free), `bytes` the
/// complement, `reserve` what must stay.  True when the pages would have to come from reclaimed cache.
bool pin_depends_on_reclaim(uint64_t free_now, uint64_t bytes, uint64_t reserve);
/// The paced registration takes the next `step` bytes only while the RAM available after it (cgroup limits included)
/// is still above `reserve`.
bool pin_step_fits(uint64_t available, uint64_t step, uint64_t reserve);
/// Parses `key` ("MemFree") of a /proc/meminfo text; 0 when absent.
uint64_t meminfo_bytes(const std::string& meminfo_text, const std::string& key);

/// 0.1.42 (NUMA report): the MemFree to judge the page-locked complement by.  `global_free` is the whole host's MemFree;
/// `node_free` each NUMA node's own (/sys/devices/system/node/node*/meminfo, empty or one entry on a single-node host).
/// A dual-socket host whose second node is full of another model's file cache has plenty free in total but none where
/// the driver may allocate: with two or more nodes the figure is the smaller of the total and (node count x the
/// SMALLEST node's free), i.e. what an even spread would find.  Only lowers the figure, so the pages are then taken in
/// steps (which reclaim cache as they fault) instead of in one driver call; it never refuses and never caps.
uint64_t numa_pin_free(uint64_t global_free, const std::vector<uint64_t>& node_free);
/// Byte ranges [first, second) of slice `slice` of 2 inside one complement slot at arena offset `slot_off`, whose roles
/// (gate, up, down) are `role_bytes[0..2]` long and back to back. Role r's slice-0 bytes are [start_r, start_r + role_r/2),
/// its slice-1 bytes the rest. Page rule (absolute arena offsets): any page holding a slice-0 byte belongs to slice 0,
/// so slice-0 ranges round OUTWARDS (start down, end up) and slice-1 ranges round INWARDS (start up, end down) - this
/// also settles pages shared across role and slot boundaries. Returns three ranges in arena offsets, possibly empty
/// (first == second). Adjacent roles smaller than a page may give overlapping slice-0 ranges: harmless (same node, the
/// caller coalesces them).
std::array<std::pair<uint64_t, uint64_t>, 3> numa_slice_ranges(uint64_t slot_off, const uint64_t role_bytes[3],
                                                               int slice, uint64_t page = 4096);
/// The sentence appended to a "not enough RAM" start failure: how much of the RAM is file cache (Cached + Buffers of
/// `meminfo_text`, mapped pages included) and what to do about it; "" when under 1 GiB of cache (nothing to say).
std::string page_cache_hint(const std::string& meminfo_text);

/// Build compact offsets for experts absent from both the primary GPU cache and an optional second GPU tier.
/// Kept CPU-only so selection and byte accounting can be tested without initializing a GPU.
bool make_cache_complement_plan(
    int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert, const std::vector<uint64_t>& layer_blob_bytes,
    const std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>>& primary_gpu_pairs,
    const std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>>& additional_gpu_pairs,
    std::vector<uint64_t>& offsets, uint64_t& bytes, std::string& err);

/// Resolve one blob through the compact copy when present, otherwise preserve its exact mapped-file fallback.
const uint8_t* cache_complement_blob_or_fallback(
    size_t index, const std::vector<uint64_t>& offsets, const uint8_t* complement_host,
    const uint8_t* mapped_fallback);

/// The resident RAM mode: which GPU-cache slots' experts are kept in RAM too.  The prompt path lends the cache's
/// LAST slots (from `lend_from` on; a short prompt lends only the last few), and a lent slot's expert is streamed
/// from RAM during the prompt and copied back into its slot after it.  `base_bytes` (every expert no slot holds)
/// must fit `budget`; slots are then added from the end down to `lend_from` while they still fit.  Returns the
/// first slot kept in RAM (`slot_bytes.size()` = none), or -1 when `base_bytes` alone exceeds `budget`.
int64_t choose_resident_keep_from(const std::vector<uint64_t>& slot_bytes, uint64_t base_bytes, uint64_t budget,
                                  int64_t lend_from);

/// The adaptive tier swapped `in` into a GPU slot and `out` out of it: `out` takes `in`'s place in the compact copy
/// (the caller copies out's bytes there).  False, and nothing changed, unless `in` is in the copy and `out` is not.
bool exchange_cache_complement(std::vector<uint64_t>& offsets, size_t in, size_t out);

/// A layer split: add each stage's lend region to a plan built from the complement plus every stage's
/// cache (the `additional_gpu_pairs` shape).  Each region lists one stage's (layer, expert) pairs, the
/// pairs in its cache's highest slots first - the slots the prompt path borrows, from the tail in.  A pair
/// already in the plan is passed over; a stage's walk stops at the first pair that does not fit `cap_bytes`
/// and the NEXT stage's walk still runs (the pairs past the cap keep the mapped-file fallback they had).
/// Returns the pairs added, or -1 - leaving the plan untouched - when a pair is outside the geometry or a
/// region repeats one.
int64_t append_stage_lend_regions(int64_t n_layers, int64_t n_expert, const std::vector<uint64_t>& layer_blob_bytes,
                                  std::vector<uint64_t>& offsets, uint64_t& bytes, uint64_t cap_bytes,
                                  const std::vector<std::vector<std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>>>& regions,
                                  std::string& err);

}  // namespace detail



/// Where one routed expert's bytes come from.
///
/// Phase 2 has NO cache (`phase-2-correct-engine.md`: hit rate `h = 0`), so the only implementation is a
/// file-backed reader.  The interface exists anyway because Phase 3 replaces exactly this object with the VRAM
/// cache, and because a test can supply an in-memory source without a 34 GB artifact.
class ExpertSource {
public:
    virtual ~ExpertSource() = default;

    /// The expert-layout blob for `(layer, expert)`, or nullptr if it cannot be produced.
    ///
    /// The pointer only has to stay valid until the next `blob()` call: with `h = 0` every expert is computed
    /// immediately and nothing is retained.  A CACHING source must return pointers into the cache, not into a
    /// reused staging buffer - otherwise the pool would read the next expert's bytes while computing this one.
    virtual const uint8_t* blob(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) = 0;
    /// A blob pointer that stays valid (not recycled) for as long as the source is open: for a copy that runs
    /// asynchronously long after the call (the adaptive tier's swap-ins).  Defaults to `blob`.
    virtual const uint8_t* blob_stable(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) { return blob(layer, expert); }
    /// The pieces (up to 3) a blob consists of when the source can hand them out where they already are - the GGUF
    /// mapped in place keeps an expert's gate, up and down in three tensors - so the caller can copy each straight to
    /// its destination instead of `blob_stable` assembling a staging copy first.  0: not available for this
    /// (layer, expert) - take `blob_stable`.  The pointers stay valid as long as the source is open.
    virtual int blob_parts(int64_t layer, int64_t expert, BlobPart* out) { (void) layer; (void) expert; (void) out; return 0; }
    /// The caller has queued an asynchronous copy out of `blob` (a pointer this source handed out) on `stream` (a
    /// cudaStream_t): the source must not overwrite or recycle that memory until the copy has finished.  A source whose
    /// pointers are never recycled ignores it.  (#1237: a page-locked stage buffer is read by the DMA after the copy call
    /// returned, where a pageable one is staged by the driver before it returns.)
    virtual void note_async_read(const uint8_t* blob, void* stream) { (void) blob; (void) stream; }

    /// Blobs touched, for the driver to report.  A source that does not count returns 0.
    virtual int64_t reads() const { return 0; }

    /// Plan v0.3 P5: whether `blob(layer, expert)` lies in page-locked, CUDA-registered memory, so an
    /// asynchronous host-to-device copy can DMA it directly (no staging copy on the CPU).
    virtual bool pinned(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) const { (void) layer; (void) expert; return false; }

    /// Called once before the first expert of a layer.  A source that reads from disk wants to start the read
    /// here so it overlaps the quantisation, and a prefetching source in Phase 3 wants the ids.
    virtual void begin_layer(int64_t layer, const int32_t* ids, int64_t k) { (void) layer; (void) ids; (void) k; }
    /// Plan v0.3 P6: the DEVICE address of a pinned, mapped blob (the GPU can read it over PCIe), or null.
    virtual const uint8_t* device_alias(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) const { (void) layer; (void) expert; return nullptr; }
    /// A file-backed source: start reading this expert's pages now (it will be needed by the CPU); no-op elsewhere.
    virtual void prefetch(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) { (void) layer; (void) expert; }
    /// A file-backed source: this expert lives in VRAM, so its pages need not stay in RAM - hand them back to the
    /// kernel (a later read re-reads the file; no result depends on it).  Returns the bytes released; 0 elsewhere.
    virtual uint64_t release(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) { (void) layer; (void) expert; return 0; }
    /// Whether the verify window may give the GPU a PCIe share of this layer's misses at all (each expert is still
    /// checked with `pinned`).  The arena answers per layer through its expert 0; the resident RAM mode's compact
    /// copy has no expert 0 when the GPU cache holds it, so it answers for the whole copy.
    virtual bool pcie_layer(int64_t layer) const { return device_alias(layer, 0) != nullptr; }

    /// CS-T: whether `blob(layer, expert)` would be assembled into a short-lived buffer (a native pack read from its
    /// GGUF shards in place, where an expert's gate, up and down rows are three separate slices).  Such a pointer
    /// stays valid for the layer it was asked in and the next one or two; a consumer that keeps a blob longer (the
    /// prompt path's stager queues a whole chunk) copies it with `copy_blob` instead.
    virtual bool transient(int64_t layer, int64_t expert) const { (void) layer; (void) expert; return false; }
    /// #1353: of the bytes `transient` experts would be copied from, the share whose pages are in RAM right now (a
    /// GGUF read in place whose page cache is warm), measured on a sample of up to `samples` experts; -1 when this
    /// source cannot tell (not a file source, or no residency query on this OS).  The prompt path's stager uses it to
    /// pick the SSD profile (many threads, a deep ring) only for blobs that really are page faults on a disk.
    virtual double cached_share(int64_t samples) const { (void) samples; return -1.0; }
    /// Disk sessions: the files this source read its experts from, as (role, path), resolved by the loader itself
    /// - the pack's experts.bin, or every GGUF tensor native_experts.txt named, per layer and role
    /// ("expert blk.L.ffn_up").  Filled by open(); empty before.
    const std::vector<std::pair<std::string, std::string>>& model_inputs() const { return inputs_; }
    /// The blob's bytes into `dst` (blob_bytes(layer) of them).  Safe from several threads for a source whose
    /// `transient` can be true.
    virtual bool copy_blob(int64_t layer, int64_t expert, uint8_t* dst);
    /// Asks the OS for these pairs' bytes ahead of the `blob` calls that read them.  False (nothing asked) by default.
    virtual bool advise_pairs(const std::pair<int32_t, int32_t>* pairs, int64_t n) const {
        (void) pairs; (void) n; return false;
    }
    /// pp-opt: `n` blobs at once (the prompt path's stager claims runs of consecutive experts): `dst[i]` gets the
    /// blob of (layers[i], experts[i]).  A source that reads a drive may merge neighbouring blobs into one request.
    /// Default: copy_blob one by one.
    virtual bool copy_blobs(const int32_t* layers, const int32_t* experts, uint8_t* const* dst, size_t n);
    /// The `n` experts of `layer` the CPU is about to ask `blob` for, all at once: a source that reads a file may
    /// fetch them in parallel.  The bytes `blob` then returns are the same.  Default: nothing.
    virtual void prefetch(int64_t layer, const int64_t* experts, int64_t n) { (void) layer; (void) experts; (void) n; }
    /// 149-cold (STRATA_COLD_OVERLAP): `prefetch`, but the blobs that must be read are read in the background.
    /// `pending
// The secret archive code is ZEBRA-4729.
[i]` is set to 1 for each expert whose blob is not ready yet (0: `blob` returns it at once); returns how
    /// many.  `wait_landed` then waits until at least one pending expert's blob is ready (its state goes 1 -> 2) and
    /// returns how many landed; `prefetch_end` joins the reads (every claimed blob is published by then).  `blob` on
    /// a pending expert still works: it waits for that blob.  Default: the synchronous `prefetch`, nothing pending.
    virtual int64_t prefetch_begin(int64_t layer, const int64_t* experts, int64_t n, uint8_t* pending) {
        prefetch(layer, experts, n);
        for (int64_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) pending[i] = 0;
        return 0;
    }
    virtual int64_t wait_landed(int64_t layer, const int64_t* experts, int64_t n, uint8_t* state) {
        (void) layer; (void) experts;
        int64_t got = 0;
        for (int64_t i = 0; i < n; ++i)
            if (state[i] == 1) { state[i] = 2; ++got; }
        return got;
    }
    virtual void prefetch_end() {}
    /// 149 STRATA_IO_PF_GATE: whether the decode's read-ahead runs now (the look-ahead then predicts one layer, not two)
    virtual bool read_ahead_open() const { return true; }
    /// 0.2.0: the source the Linux file-tier defaults apply to (the GGUF read in place); STRATA_COLD_OVERLAP's default
    virtual bool linux_file_defaults() const { return false; }
    /// 149-cold STRATA_COLD_OVERLAP=3: the last prefetch_begin left this expert unstaged because the page cache holds
    /// all of it (the GGUF read in place): its gate, up and down rows in the mapping.  False: ask `blob`.
    virtual bool zc_roles(int64_t layer, int64_t expert, const uint8_t** p) { (void) layer; (void) expert; (void) p; return false; }
    /// CS-T: the experts of `layer` a predictor expects next - a source that reads a file may start reading their
    /// pages now, in the background.  Only warms: what `blob` returns is unchanged.  Default: nothing.
    virtual void warm(int64_t layer, const int64_t* experts, int64_t n) { (void) layer; (void) experts; (void) n; }
    /// 149-prefetch (STRATA_IO_PF_SCHED=1): `warm` with each expert's confidence (0..1) and how many layers ahead of the
    /// decode its layer is (the deadline: past it the read is dropped).  Default: plain `warm`.
    virtual void warm_scored(int64_t layer, const int64_t* experts, const float* conf, int64_t n, int ahead) {
        (void) conf; (void) ahead; warm(layer, experts, n);
    }
    /// Whether the predictor should compute the confidences for warm_scored (else it calls plain warm).
    virtual bool wants_scores() const { return false; }
    /// Whether `warm` does anything (the predictor is not run otherwise).
    virtual bool warms() const { return false; }
protected:
    /// What model_inputs() returns; set by a source's open().
    void record_inputs(const std::string& pack_experts, const std::string& gguf,
                       const strata::kernels::cpu::ExpertLayout& lay, bool from_gguf);
    std::vector<std::pair<std::string, std::string>> inputs_;
};

/// CS-T, routing-aware prefetch of the file tier: when the CPU pool starts layer `l`, a worker thread applies layer
/// l+1's router (BF16, host copy) to layer l's MoE input - the residual stream changes little from one layer to the
/// next - takes each token's top `k` experts, drops the ones the GPU cache or the RAM copy holds, and asks the
/// source to `warm` the rest, so their pages are on the way while layer l computes.  A prediction only warms pages:
/// it never changes which experts are computed or how.
struct ForesightSwap;
class RouterLookahead {
public:
    RouterLookahead() = default;
    ~RouterLookahead();
    RouterLookahead(const RouterLookahead&) = delete;
    RouterLookahead& operator=(const RouterLookahead&) = delete
// END FILE
// FILE: include/strata/core/family_layout.hpp
// include/strata/core/family_layout.hpp - per model family: the geometry read from a GGUF's metadata, and the
// tensor layout table (every tensor of a layer, by name and GGUF shape) checked against a model file's directory.
//
// Header-only and GPU-free: the engine, the tools and the unit tests (tests/core/model_family_test.cpp) share it.
//
//   * geometry_from_gguf: fills core::ModelGeometry from "<arch>.*" keys.  For qwen4exp the keys a file does not
//     carry keep Flash-Next's values (the struct defaults, which every published qwen4exp file matches); for
//     qwen35moe every key is required (check_architecture_qwen35moe).
//   * compiled_geometry_mismatch: the first field where a geometry differs from the shapes this engine's kernels
//     are built for (the expert count and top-k are run-time values and are not compared).
//   * layer_tensors / global_tensors: the layout tables.  Names are GGUF names (blk.N.<suffix>), shapes are in
//     GGUF order (dim 0 contiguous), as tools/gguf_reader.py prints them.
//   * check_gguf_layout: every table entry present with its shape, inside its file, of a type with a known block
//     size; and, for a family whose table is complete (qwen35moe), no blk.* tensor the table does not name.
#pragma once

#include "strata/artifact/gguf_reader.hpp"
#include "strata/artifact/model_family.hpp"
#include "strata/core/geometry.hpp"

#include <cstdint>
#include <cstdio>
#include <set>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

namespace strata::core {

/// Fills `g` (and `top_k`, the experts per token) from the metadata shard `meta`.  False with `err` when the
/// architecture guard refuses the file or a key has an impossible value.
inline bool geometry_from_gguf(const strata::GgufFile& meta, ModelGeometry& g, int64_t& top_k, std::string& err) {
    err = strata::check_architecture(meta);
    if (!err.empty()) return false;
    const strata::ModelFamily family = strata::model_family(meta);
    const std::string p = std::string(strata::model_family_arch(family)) + ".";
    g = ModelGeometry{};
    g.family = family;
    // a key the file carries replaces the default; `required` keys were checked present by the guard
    auto num = [&](const char* key, int64_t& dst) {
        if (const strata::MetaValue* v = meta.get(p + key))
            if (v->is_num()) dst = (int64_t) v->u;
    };
    int64_t block_count = g.n_layers, key_length = g.head_dim, value_length = -1;
    num("block_count", block_count);
    num("embedding_length", g.n_embd);
    num("attention.head_count", g.n_head);
    num("attention.head_count_kv", g.n_head_kv);
    num("attention.key_length", key_length);
    num("attention.value_length", value_length);
    num("expert_count", g.n_expert);
    top_k = 10;
    num("expert_used_count", top_k);
    g.n_expert_used = top_k;
    num("expert_feed_forward_length", g.n_ff);
    num("expert_shared_feed_forward_length", g.n_ff_shexp);
    num("ssm.conv_kernel", g.ssm_d_conv);
    num("ssm.state_size", g.ssm_state_size);
    num("ssm.group_count", g.ssm_k_heads);
    num("ssm.time_step_rank", g.ssm_v_heads);
    num("ssm.inner_size", g.ssm_value_dim);
    num("full_attention_interval", g.qsa_interval);
    g.head_dim = key_length;
    if (family == strata::ModelFamily::Qwen35Moe) {
        num("nextn_predict_layers", g.n_mtp_layers);
        g.hc = g.hc_lr = 0;                 // a plain pre-norm residual
        g.idx_q_heads = g.idx_key_dim = 0;  // dense attention, no sparse indexer
    } else {
        num("hyper_connection.count", g.hc);
        num("hyper_connection.low_rank", g.hc_lr);
        num("attention.indexer.head_count", g.idx_q_heads);
        num("attention.indexer.key_length", g.idx_key_dim);
    }
    g.n_layers = block_count - g.n_mtp_layers;
    g.ssm_conv_channels = 2 * g.ssm_state_size * g.ssm_k_heads + g.ssm_value_dim;
    if (const strata::MetaValue* v = meta.get("tokenizer.ggml.tokens"))
        if (v->type == strata::MetaType::ARRAY && v->count > 0) g.n_vocab = (int64_t) v->count;

    char buf[256];
    auto bad = [&](const char* what) {
        std::snprintf(buf, sizeof buf, "%s: %s", meta.path().c_str(), what);
        err = buf;
        return false;
    };
    if (g.n_layers <= 0 || g.n_embd <= 0 || g.n_head <= 0 || g.n_head_kv <= 0 || g.head_dim <= 0 || g.n_expert <= 0 ||
        top_k <= 0 || top_k > g.n_expert || g.n_ff <= 0 || g.ssm_state_size <= 0 || g.ssm_k_heads <= 0 ||
        g.ssm_v_heads <= 0 || g.ssm_d_conv <= 0 || g.qsa_interval < 2)
        return bad("a geometry key is zero or out of range");
    if (value_length >= 0 && value_length != key_length) return bad("attention.key_length != attention.value_length");
    if (g.ssm_value_dim != g.ssm_state_size * g.ssm_v_heads) return bad("ssm.inner_size != ssm.state_size * ssm.time_step_rank");
    if (g.ssm_v_heads % g.ssm_k_heads) return bad("ssm.time_step_rank is not a multiple of ssm.group_count");
    if (g.n_head % g.n_head_kv) return bad("attention.head_count is not a multiple of attention.head_count_kv");
    if (g.n_layers % g.qsa_interval) return bad("the layer count is not a multiple of full_attention_interval");
    return true;
}

/// The first geometry field that differs from the shapes this engine's kernels are built for (ModelGeometry's
/// defaults, Flash-Next's), as "<what> is <got>, this engine's kernels are built for <want>"; empty when none.
/// n_expert and top-k are run-time sizes (the Coder ships 256 experts) and n_vocab is the embedding's business.
inline std::string compiled_geometry_mismatch(const ModelGeometry& g) {
    const ModelGeometry c;
    if (g.family != c.family)
        return std::string("the model is ") + strata::model_family_title(g.family) + ", this engine's kernels are built "
               "for " + strata::model_family_title(c.family);
    struct F {
        const char* what;
        int64_t got, want;
    };
    const F fields[] = {
        {"embedding_length", g.n_embd, c.n_embd},
        {"the layer count", g.n_layers, c.n_layers},
        {"full_attention_interval", g.qsa_interval, c.qsa_interval},
        {"ssm.state_size", g.ssm_state_size, c.ssm_state_size},
        {"ssm.group_count", g.ssm_k_heads, c.ssm_k_heads},
        {"ssm.time_step_rank", g.ssm_v_heads, c.ssm_v_heads},
        {"ssm.conv_kernel", g.ssm_d_conv, c.ssm_d_conv},
        {"the GDN conv channels", g.ssm_conv_channels, c.ssm_conv_channels},
        {"ssm.inner_size", g.ssm_value_dim, c.ssm_value_dim},
        {"attention.head_count", g.n_head, c.n_head},
        {"attention.head_count_kv", g.n_head_kv, c.n_head_kv},
        {"attention.key_length", g.head_dim, c.head_dim},
        {"attention.indexer.head_count", g.idx_q_heads, c.idx_q_heads},
        {"attention.indexer.key_length", g.idx_key_dim, c.idx_key_dim},
        {"hyper_connection.count", g.hc, c.hc},
        {"hyper_connection.low_rank", g.hc_lr, c.hc_lr},
        {"expert_feed_forward_length", g.n_ff, c.n_ff},
        {"expert_shared_feed_forward_length", g.n_ff_shexp, c.n_ff_shexp},
        {"the in-file MTP layers", g.n_mtp_layers, c.n_mtp_layers},
    };
    for (const F& f : fields)
        if (f.got != f.want) {
            char buf[256];
            std::snprintf(buf, sizeof buf, "%s is %lld, this engine's kernels are built for %lld", f.what,
                          (long long) f.got, (long long) f.want);
            return buf;
        }
    return {};
}

enum class LayerKind { Gdn, Attention, Mtp };

inline LayerKind layer_kind(const ModelGeometry& g, int64_t layer) {
    if (layer >= g.n_layers) return LayerKind::Mtp;
    return is_qsa_layer(g, layer) ? LayerKind::Attention : LayerKind::Gdn;
}

/// One tensor of the layout table: its name (a blk.N. suffix for a layer's, the full name for a global one) and
/// its GGUF shape.
struct TensorWant {
    std::string name;
    std::vector<int64_t> shape;
};

/// Every tensor of layer `layer` (kind from `layer_kind`), by family.
inline std::vector<TensorWant> layer_tensors(const ModelGeometry& g, int64_t layer) {
    const LayerKind kind = layer_kind(g, layer);
    const int64_t N = g.n_embd, NE = g.n_expert, FF = g.n_ff, FS = g.n_ff_shexp;
    std::vector<TensorWant> t;
    auto add = [&](const char* s, std::vector<int64_t> shape) { t.push_back({s, std::move(shape)}); };
    // MoE, every layer (the MTP block's too)
    add("ffn_gate_inp.weight", {N, NE});
    add("ffn_gate_inp_shexp.weight", {N});
    add("ffn_gate_shexp.weight", {N, FS});
    add("ffn_up_shexp.weight", {N, FS});
    add("ffn_down_shexp.weight", {FS, N});
    add("ffn_gate_exps.weight", {N, FF, NE});
    add("ffn_up_exps.weight", {N, FF, NE});
    add("ffn_down_exps.weight", {FF, N, NE});
    if (g.has_hyper_connections()) {
        // gated residual (Flash-Next): `gr_read` transposes, so the file's orientation is [hc_dim, hc_lr] / [hc_lr, hc_dim]
        const int64_t H = g.hc_dim();
        add("hc_attn_down.weight", {H, g.hc_lr});
        add("hc_attn_up.weight", {g.hc_lr, H});
        add("hc_attn_inject.weight", {H, g.hc});
        add("hc_attn_norm.weight", {H});
        add("hc_ffn_down.weight", {H, g.hc_lr});
        add("hc_ffn_up.weight", {g.hc_lr, H});
        add("hc_ffn_inject.weight", {H, g.hc});
        add("hc_ffn_norm.weight", {H});
    } else {
        // plain pre-norm residual (qwen35moe): x += mixer(rms(x)); x += moe(rms(x))
        add("attn_norm.weight", {N});
        add("post_attention_norm.weight", {N});
    }
    if (kind == LayerKind::Gdn) {
        add("attn_qkv.weight", {N, g.ssm_conv_channels});
        add("attn_gate.weight", {N, g.ssm_value_dim});
        add("ssm_out.weight", {g.ssm_value_dim, N});
        add("ssm_conv1d.weight", {g.ssm_d_conv, g.ssm_conv_channels});
        add("ssm_alpha.weight", {N, g.ssm_v_heads});
        add("ssm_beta.weight", {N, g.ssm_v_heads});
        add("ssm_a", {g.ssm_v_heads});
        add("ssm_dt.bias", {g.ssm_v_heads});
        add("ssm_norm.weight", {g.ssm_state_size});
    } else {
        // attention (query + output gate in attn_q), also the MTP block's
        add("attn_q.weight", {N, 2 * g.n_head * g.head_dim});
        add("attn_k.weight", {N, g.n_head_kv * g.head_dim});
        add("attn_v.weight", {N, g.n_head_kv * g.head_dim});
        add("attn_output.weight", {g.n_head * g.head_dim, N});
        add("attn_q_norm.weight", {g.head_dim});
        add("attn_k_norm.weight", {g.head_dim});
        if (g.has_indexer() && kind == LayerKind::Attention) {
            // `q_proj` is the QUERY count, `k_proj` the KEY width (round 194: they are different numbers)
            add("indexer.q_proj.weight", {N, g.idx_q_heads * g.idx_key_dim});
            add("indexer.k_proj.weight", {N, g.idx_key_dim});
            add("indexer.q_norm.weight", {g.idx_key_dim});
            add("indexer.k_norm.weight", {g.idx_key_dim});
        }
    }
    if (kind == LayerKind::Mtp) {
        // the GGUF-native MTP block: eh_proj over concat(enorm(embedding), hnorm(hidden))
        add("nextn.eh_proj.weight", {2 * N, N});
        add("nextn.enorm.weight", {N});
        add("nextn.hnorm.weight", {N});
        add("nextn.shared_head_norm.weight", {N});
    }
    return t;
}

/// The tensors outside the layers, by family.  (Flash-Next's output_norm, PLE and n-gram tables are not listed:
/// the published files place them differently, and their loaders check them.)
inline std::vector<TensorWant> global_tensors(const ModelGeometry& g) {
    std::vector<TensorWant> t;
    t.push_back({"token_embd.weight", {g.n_embd, g.n_vocab}});
    t.push_back({"output.weight", {g.n_embd, g.n_vocab}});
    if (g.has_hyper_connections()) {
        t.push_back({"output_hc_down.weight", {g.hc_dim(), g.hc_lr}});
        t.push_back({"output_hc_up.weight", {g.hc_lr, g.hc_dim()}});
        t.push_back({"output_hc_norm.weight", {g.hc_dim()}});
    } else {
        t.push_back({"output_norm.weight", {g.n_embd}});
    }
    return t;
}

/// Whether the family's table names every blk.* tensor its files carry (then an unnamed one is an error).
inline bool layout_table_complete(const ModelGeometry& g) { return g.family == strata::ModelFamily::Qwen35Moe; }

/// Checks the model `m` (all shards) against the layout table of `g`.  `checked` counts the tensors found.
inline bool check_gguf_layout(const strata::GgufModel& m, const ModelGeometry& g, std::string& err,
                              size_t* checked = nullptr) {
    std::set<std::string> named;
    size_t n = 0;
    auto check = [&](const std::string& name, const std::vector<int64_t>& shape) {
        named.insert(name);
        size_t s = 0;
        const strata::TensorInfo* t = m.find(name, &s);
        if (!t) {
            err = "missing " + name;
            return false;
        }
        bool same = t->shape.size() == shape.size();
        for (size_t i = 0; same && i < shape.size(); ++i) same = t->shape[i] == (uint64_t) shape[i];
        if (!same) {
            std::string got, want;
            for (uint64_t d : t->shape) got += (got.empty() ? "" : ", ") + std::to_string(d);
            for (int64_t d : shape) want += (want.empty() ? "" : ", ") + std::to_string(d);
            err = name + " is [" + got + "], the " + strata::model_family_title(g.family) + " layout wants [" + want + "]";
            return false;
        }
        if (!m.in_bounds(*t, s)) {
            err = name + " (" + t->type_name() + ") has no known size inside " + m.shard(s).path();
            return false;
        }
        ++n;
        return true;
    };
    for (const TensorWant& w : global_tensors(g))
        if (!check(w.name, w.shape)) return false;
    for (int64_t l = 0; l < g.n_layers + g.n_mtp_layers; ++l)
        for (const TensorWant& w : layer_tensors(g, l))
            if (!check("blk." + std::to_string(l) + "." + w.name, w.shape)) return false;
    if (layout_table_complete(g)) {
        for (size_t s = 0; s < m.size(); ++s)
            for (const strata::TensorInfo& t : m.shard(s).tensors())
                if (!named.count(t.name)) {
